# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file. Everything hand-written under
`## Unreleased` is kept; each released section below it is generated by `scripts/release.mjs`.

---

## Unreleased

### Notes

## 2.2.3 — 2026-10-21

### Notes

**A patch release: the gateways judge configuration, a new field and a new table one way, and a bootstrap or an upgrade
keeps your own SessionStart hooks.** A configuration record on an existing table, its script included, is baseline
configuration, unless §1.1 lists it. A new field on a baseline table is an extension. Any new table is a custom object
and waits for approval. The ITSM gateway also checks for the business rule that fills an incident's assignment group
from its configuration item; it ships with a plugin, so the gateway verifies the instance has it. Your own SessionStart
hooks in `.claude/settings.local.json` now survive a bootstrap and an upgrade.

### Fixed

- A bootstrap or an upgrade replaced every SessionStart hook in `.claude/settings.local.json` with its own, so a
  SessionStart hook of yours was lost each time. It now rewrites only the hook it wrote and keeps yours (ARC-09-C193).
- The doctor's store check (SV-02) printed a store key path as a masked name: an unknown key under the instance `pdi`
  read `i***.p*** Unrecognized key(s) in object: 'notes'`. The server now names the path in backticks, which the doctor
  prints as written (ARC-09-C211).

### Changed

- The six gateways define Verdict B one way: a new field on a baseline table, and nothing else. A configuration record
  on an existing table, such as an assignment rule, rows of an existing data lookup, a business rule, a notification or
  an ACL rule, is Verdict A, its script included, unless it is itself a §1.1 object. Any new table, one that extends a
  baseline table included, is Verdict C and waits for approval, where a gateway could treat a table extension as B and
  proceed. FSO's service definitions, decision-table rows, document lists and record producers move from B to A. The
  ITSM gateway grounds assignment in the **Populate Assignment Group based on CI/SO** business rule and in the
  conditions and Execution Order of assignment rules (ARC-09-C204).
- The platform notes no longer name the maintainers' own instances (ARC-09-C216).

### Internal

- The upgrade test harness gives every product process it starts a home of its own, so a test run neither reads nor rewrites
  the developer's `~/.claude.json` (ARC-09-C212).

### Fixed

- engine: ARC-09-C204, the routing tables and after-gateway lines spare an A the TD (ec66f1a)
- engine: ARC-09-C204, every gateway's Part 4 names its letter, and A needs no TD (bb7e608)
- bootstrap: ARC-09-C193, a user's session-start.mjs outside our tree is theirs (6d178da)
- bootstrap: ARC-09-C193, a bootstrap replaces only our SessionStart hook, not the key (59c931c)
- server: ARC-09-C211, a store error names its key path in backticks (496c826)
- engine: ARC-09-C204, B is a new field only, configuration is A, any new table is C (1a5423b)

## 2.2.2 — 2026-10-21

### Notes

**A patch release: an upgrade that stopped finishes on the next run, and the doctor reports what it finds instead of
crashing on it.** An upgrade interrupted after it moved the tree, by a Ctrl-C during its bootstrap or a bootstrap that
ended early, no longer says "up to date" on the next run: `./snowarch upgrade` names the steps still to run and finishes
them. Right after an upgrade from a tree with no release tag in its history, the doctor no longer reports the release
just installed as available. The upgrade and the doctor's release check ask git with its prompts off, so an origin that
wants a credential no stored helper holds fails at once with a line that says so, instead of waiting on a prompt nobody
sees. An install whose origin is another GitHub repository is told, where its upgrade stops, the command that points it
at `https://github.com/farstic/snowarch.git`.

The doctor no longer crashes when `engine.config.json` lacks a key it requires: its configuration check names the key
and how to restore the file, and any command that meets an error it did not expect prints one line, with the details
under `--verbose`. A remote the doctor cannot reach is a warning with git's own reason, where it was a failed check that
made the doctor exit 1. Settings and package names in printed lines read as written again: `docs.pin`, `http.proxy` and
the Windows remedy `winget install Git.Git` had been masked as if they were people's names. The next bootstrap no longer
runs a step again because that step changed one of its own inputs, as the instance step does when it migrates the store.

The server is unchanged: its contract keeps its 399 tools and 75 error codes, the always-loaded rule file is 73 lines,
and the licence format is unchanged. The signed manifest covers the same 455 files. A 2.2.1 install upgrades with
`./snowarch upgrade`.

### Fixed

- `./snowarch upgrade` and the doctor's release-currency check (E-28) ask git with its prompts off. An origin that
  wanted a credential no stored helper held, or that answered as a private or missing repository, could wait on a
  prompt nobody saw: up to 120 seconds in the upgrade, 15 in the doctor. It now stops at once: the upgrade with
  `upgrade: origin asked for credentials, or was not found`, the doctor's E-28 with a warning,
  `origin asked for credentials, or was not found`, and git's own `fatal:` line under it; a credential a helper stores
  still applies (ARC-09-C190).
- The doctor's release-currency check (E-28) reported an origin it could not ask for its tags as `check crashed`, at
  status FAIL, so the doctor exited 1: an origin that wanted a credential, was not found, did not resolve, or sat
  behind a proxy. It now warns with the reason the upgrade gives and git's own line under it, and records no check that
  did not happen. A server's plain 404, git's `repository '…' not found`, is now named as not found in the upgrade and
  in E-28. And the proxy remedy printed `h***.p***` for `http.proxy`, in the upgrade too; it now reads `http.proxy`
  (ARC-09-C207).
- A key named in a printed line is no longer shown as a masked name. The redactor reads a bare `word.word` as a
  person's first.last, so the doctor's configuration check (E-06) printed `d***.p*** is missing` for `docs.pin`, and
  the revocation-list line, the roster count, SK-04 and the docs family plan did the same with their keys. Those lines
  now name the key in backticks, which the redactor leaves alone. `cmd.exe` is no longer masked, and neither is the
  package id an install command names, so the Windows git remedy reads `winget install Git.Git` again instead of
  `winget install G***.G***` (ARC-09-C208).
- The doctor no longer crashes when `engine.config.json` is missing a key it requires (`docs.pin`, `docs.family`,
  `mcp.serverKey` or `floors.node`). It ended in a Node stack trace; E-06 now reports the key as missing, with its
  remedy, `git checkout -- engine.config.json`. Any other command that meets an error it did not handle prints one line
  saying what happened, and the stack only with `--verbose` (ARC-09-C209).
- Right after an upgrade from a tree with no release tag in its history, as a hand-made export is, the doctor's
  release-currency check (E-28) said the release just installed was available. It now reads its record against the
  release the tree is on, and says the tree is up to date (ARC-09-C210).
- An upgrade that stopped after moving the tree no longer reports "up to date" on the next run. A Ctrl-C during its
  bootstrap, a bootstrap that ended before it recorded a step, and a missing release manifest each left the next run
  saying the install was current; it now names the steps still to run and finishes them. A step that finished with a
  warning no longer reads "last run did not finish" in the plan (ARC-09-C196).
- A bootstrap step that changes one of its own inputs, as the instance step does when it migrates the store, is no
  longer run again by the next bootstrap when nothing else has changed. The step recorded its inputs as they were before
  it ran; it now records them as it left them (ARC-09-C213).

### Changed

- From this release, an upgrade whose origin is a GitHub repository other than the one releases are published at says
  so where it stops, at the fetch or at a signed manifest that names another commit, and gives the `git remote set-url`
  command that points it at `https://github.com/farstic/snowarch.git`. An origin on another host is left alone, and a
  credential in the URL is never printed (ARC-09-C185c).

### Internal

- The release writes the server package's section in `packages/snowarch/CHANGELOG.md` at a final, as it does for this
  file, and carries the open "Known gap" paragraphs forward. A final whose server package changed with nothing written
  there is refused before anything is written; the 2.2.1 cut was such a final (ARC-09-C206).
- The line U5 prints after a move to a release before 2.2.1 names a fresh install (`docs/MIGRATION.md`) instead of an
  `upgrade` that release's own code refuses. Only a checkout whose origin carries those tags can make that move
  (ARC-09-C203).
- C207's credential case no longer fails when a Windows runner stalls the start of git's HTTP helper past E-28's
  budget. It starts the helper once before the measured call, and accepts the timeout only when git's trace proves the
  start stalled (ARC-09-C214).
- The Windows upgrade check runs the run-after-a-move cases in their own file, in its second shard: the first shard had
  reached its 45-minute limit with every case passing (ARC-09-C215).

## 2.2.1 — 2026-10-20

### Notes

**A patch release: a refused write says why, a missing mandatory field is caught before anything is written, and the
first ITSM tools are proven on an instance.** When the instance refuses a write because of its own data policy or
business rule, the error now has its own code, `INSTANCE_REFUSED`. Its remedy is to correct the record, so the
Architect no longer advises a change of roles against the instance's own words. Before the first write, every create
reads the data policies of its table. A field one requires that the call would not send is refused there, naming the
policy, so the Architect asks for the value instead of stopping halfway through a build. A policy that applies only
under a condition is named as possibly required, and the write goes ahead. Resolving an incident or a
problem now stores the resolution time, not midnight. A read that asks for fields names any the instance did not
return, and the upgrade names the version it starts from as the doctor does. The first ITSM run against a real instance
verified adding, modifying and querying incidents: 5 of the server's 399 tools are now verified live, and the run found
the resolve defect fixed here. The Architect also knows more about AI Agent Studio:
- an agent is switched on by a record modify;
- the Studio access-rules step is a write with its own approval;
- a browser already open needs a new login after capture moves;
- a net-new AI agent needs the Prime tier.

The server's contract keeps its 399 tools and gains one error code, 75 in all. The always-loaded rule file is 73 lines,
under its cap of 80. The licence format is unchanged. The signed manifest now covers every file a release ships, 455
of them: governance, templates, the scripts the Architect runs and the user documentation joined it, so the doctor
restores any of them as it restores the engine. From this release the Architect is installed and upgraded from
`https://github.com/farstic/snowarch`, where the Release and its evidence are published. An install of 2.2.0 or earlier
checks a release by its own older rule and refuses this one, changing nothing, so it is replaced once by a fresh install
that carries over the licence, the instance store and the engagement folders. An install of a 2.2.1 release candidate
moves with `git remote set-url origin https://github.com/farstic/snowarch.git`, then `./snowarch upgrade` (MIGRATION.md
has both).

### Fixed

- Incident and problem resolve store `resolved_at` with its time, in the instance's format and in UTC. They sent an
  ISO string, which the instance stored as the date alone, at midnight (ARC-09-C184).
- The query guard accepts every documented GlideSystem `…Ago` function, the `…Start`, `…End` and `…Local` forms
  included. Any other `javascript:` expression is still refused, and the refusal now says to compute the value and
  send it literally (ARC-09-C172).
- `./snowarch upgrade` names the version it upgrades from by the doctor's rule — the release tag, the tag and the
  commits past it, or `a development tree at <sha>` — and never an `import/*` tag (ARC-09-C170).
- `./snowarch doctor --no-network` makes no network call. The release-currency check (E-28) read the flag where the
  doctor never put it and still asked `origin` for its tags, so every report taken with `--no-network` before 2.2.1,
  the release evidence included, made that one call (ARC-09-C187).
- A release is checked against the coverage its own signed manifest carries, as the manifest's format says, not against
  the coverage of the code doing the checking. Before this, an upgrade refused a release that covers more files than the
  install, and a release push checked the same way (ARC-09-C192). The release gate also refuses a manifest signed with
  another coverage than the release's own; an install of 2.2.0 or earlier keeps its own rule and is replaced once (see
  the Notes).
- From this release, an upgrade checks every floor its target states (Claude Code, git and Node) with the bootstrap's
  own rules before it moves anything, so a release that needs a newer git or Node is refused with nothing changed, not
  after the move. `--force-floor` now completes the upgrade it allows: the bootstrap is told to skip its Claude Code
  check (ARC-09-C194).
- On Windows, `snowarch.cmd` runs the command and returns its exit code on one line, so an upgrade that replaces the
  launcher while it runs no longer leaves it reading the new file at the old place, which printed `|| was unexpected at
  this time.` or ran the command again, thousands of times when the file grew (ARC-09-C194).
- From this release, after a move from a release, the command an upgrade names to return to it is `./snowarch upgrade
  --to` that release, which checks and records it again; `git checkout` would have left the record naming the newer one
  (ARC-09-C194).
- From this release, an install on a branch moves to the release tag the upgrade verified, never past it to an
  unreleased commit, or is refused with nothing changed (ARC-09-C194).
- `Status` reads the release family and pin from the `Docs:` line of the `./snowarch status` panel. The session
  looked for a git branch in the corpus checkout, which has none, and reported the corpus as not a git checkout
  (ARC-09-C188).

### Changed

- A 403 that the instance's own rule gave — a data policy or a business rule — is `INSTANCE_REFUSED`, with the
  remedy "read the message, correct the fields it names and send the write again, as a new write with its own
  approval; do not change roles". An ACL refusal stays `INSUFFICIENT_PRIVILEGES`. The always-loaded rule file's cap
  goes from 72 to 80 lines, by the owner's ruling (ARC-09-C182).
- The write gate's approval question applies to a browser action that changes the instance, as well as to a tool call
  (ARC-09-C172).
- The Architect is installed and upgraded from https://github.com/farstic/snowarch, a repository that carries only
  what a user receives, and the signed manifest covers every file it ships (ARC-09-C185a, ARC-09-C185c).

### Added

- Before a create, the server reads the active data policies of the table and of the tables it extends, and refuses
  a field an unconditional rule requires that the create would not send. The refusal names the policy and says to
  ask the user. A conditional rule, a defaulted field and a check that could not be made go on the result as
  `mandatory_fields`. The AI agent, workflow and ATF step builds check every table before their first write
  (ARC-09-C183).
- `snow_core_record_read` and `snow_core_records_query` name, as `fields_not_returned`, the requested fields the
  instance did not return (ARC-09-C178).
- The Architect knows the AI Agent Studio steps from the owner's live run: activation through
  `snow_core_record_modify`, the access-rules step in the browser as a write with its own approval, the new login a
  browser needs after capture moves, and what Studio's save does by itself. The Licensing Specialist names the Prime
  tier for a net-new AI agent (ARC-09-C172).

### Internal

- Platform notes PN-19 to PN-23: Studio's access-rules step, a Protected Script Include's script, a mandatory field
  over the REST Table API, a date-time sent as an ISO string, and the instance's own close codes. PN-17 now gives the
  unnamed tool link's refusal as a data policy (ARC-09-C172, ARC-09-C183, ARC-09-C184).
- `docs/validation/2026-10-19-itsm-sweep.md`, the first ITSM run (ARC-09-C167b, group 1, partial). The live-coverage
  ledger moves to 5 tools verified live.
- `scripts/distribution.mjs` checks, exports and stages the distribution tree; the contract gate's generated step runs
  its check (ARC-09-C185a).
- `scripts/release.mjs --tag-only --push` pushes a final from 2.2.1 only with `--distribution` and a clone of
  farstic/snowarch whose tag is that tag staged and whose manifest is signed from it. `release.yml` installs, judges and
  measures the staged tree on three OSes and compares the distribution tag's tree. An rc keeps the gate it had
  (ARC-09-C185b).

Tag v2.2.1 · contract 2eb5b9945857 · docs-pin aa447ce

## 2.2.0 — 2026-10-17

### Notes

**A minor release: the AI agent tools write to AI Agent Studio's own tables, and the Architect designs an agentic
solution with its access rules and its build plan.** The four AI agent tools wrote to tables a current instance does
not have. They now write an agent, its tool links and a workflow's team, use case and members where AI Agent Studio
keeps them, read every record back, refuse before writing what they cannot check, and create no access control: their
result says so and names the Studio page where it is configured. Two of them take different inputs, which makes this
release breaking for a caller of those two tools. The Now Assist Specialist knows that model now: an agent's tool is
an AI Agent Studio tool, every agent and workflow design states its access rules, and the design ends with a build
plan that asks one write question per call, naming every record the call writes, which the write gate states too. A
new ledger records which of the server's 399 tools have run against a real instance: 26 are named in a validation
record or a platform note, none is verified live yet, and the contract gate keeps every tool listed. The rewritten AI
agent tools are run against an instance in the release candidate's live sitting before this version is tagged.
Whether the session-start Mode line reaches every session in a live install is still to be measured. The server's
contract keeps its 399 tools and 74 error codes, and the licence format and the signed manifest are unchanged.
Upgrading from 2.1.2 needs no step beyond `./snowarch upgrade`; a script or prompt that called the two changed tools
with their removed inputs needs the new ones.

### Breaking

- `snow_ai_ai_agent_add` no longer takes `capabilities` or `auto_generate_acls`, and requires `role` and
  `instructions`. `snow_ai_agentic_workflow_add` no longer takes `agent_sys_id`, `steps` or `trigger_conditions`, and
  requires `base_plan` and `agent_sys_ids`. Neither creates an ACL: access is configured in AI Agent Studio
  (ARC-09-C165).

### Fixed

- The AI agent tools write AI Agent Studio's tables: an agent as `sn_aia_agent` with one `sn_aia_agent_tool_m2m` per
  tool, and a workflow as `sn_aia_team`, `sn_aia_usecase` and one `sn_aia_team_member` per agent. They read back the
  configuration and version the instance creates by itself and report them; an execution mode is accepted only when
  the instance lists it; a refusal says "Nothing was written."; a failure after the first record names every record
  created and deletes nothing. Until now they wrote tables a current instance does not have (ARC-09-C165).
- The Now Assist Specialist designs an agentic solution from AI Agent Studio's model. An agent's tool is an AI Agent
  Studio tool, never one of this product's server tools, and a new one is configuration, custom code for the
  Developer, or — a new flow action or subflow built for the agent — a custom object that needs approval. Every agent
  and workflow design has its access rules (who may invoke it, the identity it runs as, its approved roles) and ends
  with a build manifest (ARC-09-C166).

### Changed

- The write gate: one call that writes several records is one write question that names every record the call
  writes, in order, and the update-set capture list names AI agents and agentic workflows (ARC-09-C166).

### Added

- `docs/validation/LIVE-COVERAGE.md`, generated from `docs/validation/live-coverage.json`: the status of each of the
  server's 399 tools against a real instance — verified live, refused, named in a record only, or unverified. The
  contract gate fails when a tool has no entry (ARC-09-C167a).

### Internal

- Two platform notes: PN-17, what an AI Agent Studio insert creates by itself, and PN-18, pointing REST capture at an
  update set moves the user's own session there too (ARC-09-C165).
- T-36, a two-turn validation test of an agentic design (ARC-09-C166).

### Breaking

- snow_ai_ai_agent_add no longer takes capabilities or auto_generate_acls and (3fb08dc)

### Added

- ci: ARC-09-C167a, the live-coverage ledger, generated and held by gen:check (27c3de2)

## 2.1.2 — 2026-10-16

### Notes

**A maintenance release after 2.1.1, from a colleague's 30-day licence and the owner's rc.3 and rc.4 sittings.** A
licence reads expiring only near the end of its own term, so a 30-day licence no longer reads expiring on its first
day, and a licence in force that is expiring tells the user to ask its issuer for a renewal. A gateway in review mode
says whether the artefact keeps its Envelope, and leaves the code to the Code Reviewer. The CSM and ITSM gateways
always weigh the closest baseline tables for a request about money, an audit trail or an escalation. Whether the
session-start Mode line reaches every session was measured in a design-only install, 10 sessions of 10; the
measurement in a live install is still to come. Nothing changes in the licence format, the signed manifest or the
server's contract, which has 399 tools and 74 error codes as in 2.1.1. Upgrading from 2.1.1 needs no step beyond
`./snowarch upgrade`.

### Fixed

- A licence reads expiring when its days left are at most a third of its own term, and never more than 30 days, the
  term running from its issue to its last day; a perpetual licence never does. For a licence in force that is
  expiring, doctor check E-31 and `./snowarch licence check` say "Licence <id> expires on <date> (<n> days left) —
  ask the issuer for a renewal before then", where they said to install a valid licence (ARC-09-C159).

### Changed

- A gateway in review mode checks the artefact against its own Envelope — its tables, fields and state values, and
  its Part 3 verdict — and returns exactly one of two answers: cleared, or a §1.1 violation, naming what and where.
  It is not a code review: the Code Reviewer is proposed separately (ARC-09-C160).
- The CSM and ITSM gateways' Part 2 always weighs the closest baseline tables for a request about money against an
  account, an audit trail or an escalation record — SLA records, invoices, contracts and entitlements, journal
  fields, audit history and the on-call rota — each with a citation to the bundled corpus (ARC-09-C161).

### Internal

- The session-start banner, measured: the Mode line reached 10 of 10 headless sessions in a design-only install,
  and a control session with the hook off counted 0. The measurement in a live install is pending (ARC-09-C162).

### Fixed

- engine: ARC-09-C161, CSM and ITSM Part 2 always weigh the closest money and audit tables (6cb40b3)
- engine: ARC-09-C160, a gateway's review mode says cleared or a §1.1 violation, in all six (9e8fe15)
- engine: ARC-09-C159, expiring scales with the licence's term; an expiring licence is renewed (09f3741)

### Internal

## 2.1.1 — 2026-10-16

### Notes

**A maintenance release after 2.1.0, from the owner's rc.3 sitting and a colleague's Windows install of 2.1.0, both
on 2026-10-15.** It changes no licence, no manifest and no protocol: licences, the signed manifest of the Architect's
own files and the server's contract are as in 2.1.0, with 399 tools and 74 error codes. Upgrading from 2.1.0 needs no
step beyond `./snowarch upgrade`.

### Fixed

- Doctor check E-32 names why a modified checkout cannot be restored — the checkout holds no release tag, or a changed
  file is reached through a link that leaves it — before it offers `./snowarch doctor --fix`, where it offered a
  repair that could not run (ARC-09-C149).
- `./snowarch version` and the status panel's Engine line name the version of record's own tag when it points at the
  checkout's commit, where `git describe` could name another tag on the same commit (ARC-09-C154).
- `./snowarch licence issue` prints the two commands that install the file under the one name the product reads,
  `.local/licence.json`, with the file's real name. With no licence installed, `./snowarch licence check` names a
  licence it finds in `.local/` under another name, with the command that renames it, and nothing from inside it
  (ARC-09-C156).
- The bootstrap's last block names the folder Claude Code has to start in, ``Next: run `claude` in this folder:``
  and the checkout's path, from the Node bootstrap and from both Node-free launchers. Run from outside any checkout,
  `doctor` and `status` name the install to `cd` to, where they said `<the checkout>` (ARC-09-C157).

### Changed

- A document, however short — a design note, an HLD, LLD or PDD — is the HLD/LLD Writer's, and a runbook or KBA is
  Operational Documentation's: proposed at Step 8, dispatched only after approval, and never written by the
  Architect itself (ARC-09-C152).
- The Integration Specialist's MID Server and alias citations name corpus pages that say what they cite
  (ARC-09-C153).
- `docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` names Git for Windows 2.55, which prints the same harmless "is not a commit!" warning on
  the documented clone as 2.54, and says how to see that the checkout is at the tag (ARC-09-C155).

### Internal

- The Windows upgrade e2e runs as two shards, each with its own 45-minute limit, behind the required context
  `upgrade-e2e (windows-latest)`, which needs both; main's required checks are unchanged (ARC-09-C158).

### Fixed

- engine: ARC-09-C157, the Next line names the folder for claude; doctor names the install (d7f7f9c)
- engine: ARC-09-C156, issue prints the install commands; check names a licence by another name (8dd002f)
- engine: a document is a builder's, however short — proposed at Step 8, never written in-thread (c58aac0)
- integration-specialist: the MID Server and alias citations name pages that say what they cite (7e9a394)
- engine: the engine line names the version of record's own tag when it points at HEAD (caa51e1)
- doctor: E-32 names why a restore would refuse before it offers doctor --fix (b04d795)

## 2.1.0 — 2026-10-15

### Notes

**This release moves the product to the Business Source License 1.1, and holds the AI Architect's own files to a
manifest the owner signs (ARC-11-C5, ARC-11-C7, ARC-11-C141).** Licence enforcement is as in 2.0.13.

From 2.1.0 the product is under the Business Source License 1.1 (`BUSL-1.1`), recorded in ADR-0011. `LICENSE` and
`packages/snowarch/LICENSE` carry its text and its parameters, among them the Additional Use Grant, which says
what production use it permits, and the Change Date, 2030-10-14, from which the Licensed Work is under the Apache
License 2.0. `LICENSING.md` says what this means for a user. A release before 2.1.0 keeps the licence it was
published under.

Every release from 2.1.0 carries a manifest of the Architect's own files, signed by the owner, and a change to one
of those files does not take effect: the file is put back, and one line says so. A user's own material belongs under
`clients/<name>/`.

**Upgrading from 2.0.13.** There is no migration step. The upgrade from 2.0.13 runs 2.0.13's own planning, which
predates the manifest, so it cannot check a 2.1.0 target before the tree moves; the new tree's bootstrap fetches the
manifest (B01) and E-32 then checks the checkout against it (ARC-09-C140). The server has 399 tools, as in 2.0.13,
and the error registry gains `INTEGRITY_NOT_VERIFIED`, for 74 codes.

**The release candidate, and what the owner's sitting confirmed.** `v2.1.0-rc.3` is release commit `a983c98`, cut
from develop `a9384f9`. The owner's sitting on it, on 2026-10-15, confirmed:
- the upgrade path — 2.0.13 → rc.1 on 2026-10-14, rc.1 → rc.3 and rc.3 → rc.4 on 2026-10-15 — the Business Source
  License's text, and a second install;
- a change to the Architect's own files put back with the session continuing, and a checkout that cannot be restored
  refusing, with its manifest missing and with its release's tag deleted;
- T-01, T-02, T-04 to T-10, T-14, T-15, T-22, T-24, T-27, T-32 and T-33. The remaining tests were skipped by design.

Two did not pass on rc.3. In T-02b a request for a table model was read as naming a custom table (ARC-09-C150). In
T-03 the Architect let a gateway's routing recommendation decide its routing (ARC-09-C151). `v2.1.0-rc.4` is release
commit `ca5212c`, cut from develop `8422895` after #448, which carries both fixes. The owner's check on it, the same
day, passed:
- T-02b gave Verdict A: the request names no custom object, so the verdict follows the baseline evaluation;
- T-03 proposed the Integration Specialist first, then the Flow Designer Specialist, with the Security & GRC consult,
  in the Architect's own `Proposal:` line.

### Added

- A signed manifest of the Architect's own files (ARC-11-C7): the sha256 of each file the product ships in the engine
  and the server, `CLAUDE.md`, and the rules, skills, agents and settings under `.claude/`, signed with the owner's
  key (`./snowarch licence sign-release`). A release's tag is pushed only once its manifest is published. A checkout
  fetches it at install (B01), with `./snowarch licence check --refresh`, and at upgrade, which takes a 2.1 or later
  target only with that target's own signed manifest. Doctor check E-32 compares the checkout with it, `status` names
  the result (`Integrity: verified · v2.1.0 · <n> files`), and the session banner names a failure.
- A change to the Architect's own files does not take effect (ARC-11-C141). By the owner's ruling, a licensed user
  keeps using the Architect and cannot change it. Before any command, at session start, at the server's start and
  with `./snowarch doctor --fix`, a covered file that differs from the signed manifest is put back from the release's
  tag, and anything replaced, or added under a covered folder, is moved to `.local/integrity-removed/<UTC stamp>/`,
  never deleted. One line says so, `Integrity: restored — …`, with `— restart claude` when `CLAUDE.md` or
  anything under `.claude/` was put back, and the run continues. Only a checkout that cannot be restored refuses:
  `./snowarch` runs only the commands that show and repair the state, every MCP tool answers
  `INTEGRITY_NOT_VERIFIED`, and `CLAUDE.md`'s rule produces no deliverable and points to `./snowarch doctor`.
- `./snowarch docs verify` reads a name written as words, quoted, italic or backticked outside fences, and wants it
  printed in the bundled corpus as written; the marker, or an example's own entry, answers it (ARC-09-C144).

### Changed

- The product is under the Business Source License 1.1 from 2.1.0 (ARC-11-C5, ADR-0011).
- Doctor check E-10 FAILs when the SessionStart hook that prints the `Licence:` and `Integrity:` lines is missing or
  wrong, and `./snowarch doctor --fix` registers it again (ARC-11-C10).
- The roster says what the bundled corpus says. Skill lines that named a baseline object the corpus does not print
  now carry the corpus's name and its citation, or say that it is not documented in the bundled corpus: the ITSM
  notifications and auto-close, the ATF step names, the CSM and major incident notifications, Flow Designer's *Where
  to run the flow*, and the Teams and Slack spokes' message actions (ARC-09-C144); and the engine review's corrections
  (ARC-02-C6 to C13). Figures are exported with the house scripts (ARC-02-C5).
- Verdict C only when a custom object is named in the request, or is the only viable path after the baseline
  evaluation; a request for a table model, an ACL matrix or a Script Include names none, and the verdict follows the
  evaluation (ARC-09-C150). The Architect proposes the builders in order — the Integration Specialist, then the Flow
  Designer Specialist, then the Developer — with the §3.1 consults the task triggers, in its own `Proposal:` line
  (ARC-09-C151).

### Fixed

- An upgrade's bootstrap fetches the release's signed manifest whenever the checkout does not hold it, where an
  upgrade from 2.0.13 ended with E-32's FAIL on a correct install, because nothing had fetched it (ARC-09-C140).
- The server doctor's handshake reports a server that exited before it answered, "the server exited with code <n>
  before the handshake finished", where an uncaught `EPIPE` ended the doctor (ARC-09-C130).

## 2.0.13 — 2026-10-13

### Notes

**This release brings enforcement on every build (ARC-11-C6):** with no valid licence, no tool runs, no CLI command
runs but the few that show and repair the state, and no variable turns that off. It stays under the Apache License
2.0; from 2.1.0 the product moves to the Business Source License 1.1 (ARC-11-C5).

A checkout with no licence in force (missing, invalid, expired or revoked), or with a design-only licence on a live
server, sees this:
- every MCP tool answers `LICENCE_NOT_VALID` and writes one audit line, as 2.0.12 did under `SNOW_LICENCE_ENFORCE`;
- `./snowarch` runs `licence`, `doctor`, `status`, `version`, `upgrade`, `bootstrap` and `mode design`. Every other
  command exits 3, naming the state and the remedy; the package CLI runs `doctor` only;
- doctor check E-31 is a FAIL, with the remedy every refusal carries;
- `./snowarch upgrade` says so before it moves to a release that refuses, with `--yes` and without.

`SNOW_LICENCE_ENFORCE` is gone: no variable, flag or file turns enforcement off, and setting it changes nothing. With
a licence in force nothing is refused, and only the licence line's wording changes. There is no migration step. The
server has 399 tools and the error registry 73 codes, as in 2.0.12. The contract moves to `20f73faf89cd`, because
`LICENCE_NOT_VALID`'s meaning and remedy no longer name the variable.

The no-deliverable rule reads the session banner's `Licence:` line, which the SessionStart hook prints; a session
that loads no local settings, has hooks off, or has the hook entry removed does not see it, and only the rule goes
silent — the server still refuses every MCP tool. From 2.1 the doctor fails when the hook is gone (ARC-11-C10).

**The release candidate, and what the owner's sitting confirmed.** `v2.0.13-rc.3` is release commit `5fac74b`,
cut from develop `20d05f4`, after #420 (ARC-11-C6), #424 (the sweep) and #425 (ARC-07-C43, the changelog guard).
Its release evidence reads the same on all three operating systems: 35 ok, 1 warn (SV-02), 1 fail (E-00) and
5 skip. E-31 is ok under the CI fixture licence; 2.0.12's evidence had it as its second warn. The owner's sitting
on it, on 2026-10-13, confirmed every step (`docs/validation/2.0.13-rc.3-live-sitting.md`):
- **T-28, revised for enforcement on every build**, with no variable set anywhere. With no licence, E-31 FAILs with
  its remedy. Revoked, `./snowarch licence check` says so, with its `valid until:` line in UTC, and every tool is
  refused with `LICENCE_NOT_VALID` and one audit line. With a new licence issued and installed, the tool answers
  again;
- **T-31 with a refused licence**: the Architect names the state and produces no deliverable;
- **T-29 and T-30 under a valid licence**, against rc.2's numbers and verdicts
  (`docs/validation/2.0.12-rc.2-live-sitting.md`): 1 min 47 s and 2 min 05 s.

After rc.3, and covered by unit, parity and e2e tests rather than by the sitting:
- in #426, ARC-11-C11, the list refresh's deadline in its third git call (under Fixed), and ARC-09-C133, two
  tests that waited on a slow runner;
- in rc.4, by the owner's ruling at the sitting, E-31's remedy and U4's notice end at the remedy, and `upgrade
  --to` checks the licence before it takes the target (ARC-11-C6);
- in rc.4, the banner runs the quick doctor again after a licence is installed (ARC-11-C12, under Fixed).

**The next release candidate.** `v2.0.13-rc.4` is release commit `c9c6b8d`, cut from develop `a34b845`, after #427.
Its release evidence reads as rc.3's on all three operating systems: 35 ok, 1 warn (SV-02), 1 fail (E-00) and 5 skip,
with E-31 ok under the CI fixture licence. The owner's check on it, on 2026-10-13, passed:
- two checkouts upgraded from rc.3 cleanly, each with its doctor at 40 ok, 2 warn (E-23, E-30) and 0 fail;
- with a revoked licence restored, E-31 FAILs with its remedy and nothing after it, and SV-06 skips with E-31's
  reason;
- without a licence in force, `upgrade --to` refused the target with exit 3, and nothing changed.

### Changed

- The licence line says the state alone: "warn only" and "enforced" are gone, because enforcement no longer has a
  switch (ARC-11-C6).
- A licence's day is a UTC date, and the commands say so. `licence show`, `verify` and `check` print
  `valid until: <date> (UTC, N days)`, `(UTC, last day)` on its last day, and `<date> (UTC)` once it has ended. E-31
  and a session's first answer say "last day" where they said "0 days left" (ARC-11-C6).
- `docs verify` refuses a Markdown table row with more cells than its header, outside fences. GitHub cuts a cell at
  the first unescaped pipe, inside a code span too, and drops every cell past the header's count, so such a row reads
  whole in the file and short on the page. The 33 rows that broke this are fixed (ARC-09-C126).
- `snow_core_records_query` says when a read was cut. It returns `total` (the Table API's `X-Total-Count`), `limit`,
  `offset` and `truncated`, where `count` alone was the page's length, so a list cut at its limit looked complete. It
  takes an `offset` to read the next page, and its limit's description states the real default and cap. The Architect
  reads a truncated answer to its end within the budget, or cites it with its total (CLAUDE.md and the six gateways,
  ARC-09-C125).

### Fixed

- `snow_us_capture_target_set` returns `previous`, the update set the authenticated user's capture preference named
  before the call, from the read it already made, so a procedure that points capture back afterwards no longer reads
  the preference first. It is null when the tool created the preference, or when the one it found named nothing
  (ARC-09-C127).
- `snow_us_current_update_set_read` reads the session's update set, the authenticated user's `sys_update_set`
  preference, where it listed up to five update sets in progress on the whole instance and called that current. It
  returns `session_update_set` (null with no preference, `found: false` for a set that does not exist), and the
  instance's in-progress sets as `in_progress_update_sets`; `count` and `active_update_sets` are gone (ARC-09-C128).
- A revocation-list refresh whose time runs out while it reads the file now says the list could not be reached, in
  the server and in `./snowarch licence check --refresh` and `upgrade`. It said no list was published yet, and wrote
  the cache as checked at that moment, which hid how old the cached list was. The cached list was kept either way
  (ARC-11-C11).
- After a licence is installed, the session banner runs the quick doctor again, where its Doctor line showed E-31's
  FAIL from before the install for up to a day (ARC-11-C12).

Tag v2.0.13 · contract 9c8de8f3113f · docs-pin 68c0d11

## 2.0.12 — 2026-10-12

### Notes

**This release adds a licence the product checks itself, warn only by default, and the licensing documents.** It
also changes four behaviours:
- a co-firing gateway writes its Envelope in the same turn (ARC-09-C118);
- in live mode, the Architect reads on the instance what the corpus cannot settle, before an Envelope is final
  (ARC-09-C117);
- `./snowarch upgrade` names an untracked file that would stop it, before anything moves (ARC-09-C120);
- the ATF step tool reads its inputs' limits and lists every input row of a step (ARC-09-C122).

There is no migration step: an existing checkout keeps its instance store, its preset and its flags untouched, and
the documentation corpus stays pinned at `68c0d11`. The server has 399 tools, as in 2.0.11. The error registry
gains one code (72 to 73), `LICENCE_NOT_VALID`, which only `SNOW_LICENCE_ENFORCE="true"` can raise.

A checkout with no licence and enforcement unset, which is every checkout today, sees exactly three things of the
licence:
- doctor check E-31 warns;
- the session banner and `./snowarch status` gain a `Licence:` line after the `Mode:` line;
- a session's first tool answer carries one extra block, after its first, which is unchanged.

**The licence of the code.** Every 2.0.x release, 2.0.12 included, stays under the Apache License 2.0. **2.0.13
brings enforcement on every build (ARC-11-C6):** with no valid licence, no tool runs, no CLI command runs but
the few that repair the state, and no variable turns that off. From 2.1.0 the product moves to the Business
Source License 1.1 (ARC-11-C5). A version's licence is the `LICENSE` file in its release tag (`LICENSING.md`,
§ Future versions).

**The release candidates, and what a live instance has confirmed.** `v2.0.12-rc.1` (`9a75d8e`) and
`v2.0.12-rc.2` (`18e5020`) were cut before this release.
- **rc.1, the owner's licence sitting, round 1:** two folders on one machine; the record is
  `docs/validation/2.0.12-rc.1-licence-sitting.md`. It confirmed ARC-11-C1:
  - a creator licence and a colleague's were issued and installed;
  - under enforcement a revoked licence refused every tool at the next session;
  - a design-only licence refused every tool of a live server;
  - a forged file read as invalid;
  - an end date already past could not be issued;
  - a placeholder name was refused;
  - the recovery key verified;
  - back to warn only, the notice was information.

  Its two defects were fixed before rc.2: the status panel now prints the `Licence:` line in every state, and
  every licence refusal writes one audit line.
- **rc.2, the owner's live sitting:** the creator folder, upgraded from rc.1; the record is
  `docs/validation/2.0.12-rc.2-live-sitting.md`.
  - T-29, live verification: eleven items measured from seven reads, each cited "measured on instance <label>,
    <date>", and no audit line written;
  - T-30, both Envelopes in one turn: FSO and CSM reconciled once, cited by full corpus path, in 2 min 22 s
    against 2.0.11-rc.1's 1 min 07 s;
  - the ATF step tool, live: a 2680-character script refused before any write, against its input variable's
    max_length of 2000, and the listing showing `jasmine_version` defaulted by the instance;
  - the status panel's `Licence:` line, rc.1's first defect, seen fixed;
  - the `expired` state, on 2026-10-12 once the UTC day turned: `Licence: expired · enforced` at the check, the
    tool refused with `LICENCE_NOT_VALID`, and one audit line written, the first licence-refusal line seen live;
  - round 2, on the colleague's machine: still to come; it runs on the colleague's machine after this release.

The live procedures are in `packages/snowarch/tests/live/README.md`.

**What a user sees, at the terminal.**
- **A licence.** `.local/licence.json` holds it: issued by the owner, signed with Ed25519, and checked offline
  against a signed revocation list. `./snowarch licence show` and `licence check` read it, and
  `licence verify <file>` checks any licence file.
- **Enforcement is opt-in in 2.0.12.** Without `SNOW_LICENCE_ENFORCE="true"` nothing is refused. With it:
  - a missing, invalid, expired or revoked licence refuses every MCP tool, and every CLI command but the five
    that repair the state;
  - a design-only licence refuses every tool of a live server;
  - each refused tool call writes one audit line.
- **The upgrade names what stops it.** An untracked file the release tracks, or a modified one, is named before
  anything moves, and a failure while the tree moves quotes git's reason.

**What a consultant sees, in a session.**
- **Co-firing gateways write both Envelopes in the turn.** "Reconcile before dispatch" is gone. Each Envelope
  cites as a single-gateway turn does, `(citation: markdown/<area>/<page>.md)`, and has its own eight-page
  grounding budget.
- **Live mode reads what the corpus cannot settle.**
  - Choice values, fields, table parents, installed apps, assignment rules, SLA definitions and workspace pages
    are read on the instance with read-only calls, eight per Envelope by default, and with no cap under the
    Ultracode or max-effort opt-in, and cited "measured on instance <label>, <date>".
  - The raw reads go in `<envelope>-readings.md` beside the Envelope, with the label and the date, never a URL or
    an account.
  - What is not an instance fact, such as the wording a customer sees, stays an OPEN QUESTION. Design-only is
    unchanged.
- **The ATF step tool reads its inputs' limits.** A value over its input variable's `max_length` is refused
  before any write. Every input row of the step is listed, including those the instance defaulted.
- **Under enforcement, a licence not in force stops the deliverable.** The Architect names the state and points
  to `./snowarch licence check`. Without enforcement, the `Licence:` line is information.

### Added

- `./snowarch licence`:
  - `show`, `verify <file>` and `check [--refresh]` for whoever installs a licence, as
    `.local/licence.json`;
  - `keygen`, `issue`, `init-list` and `revoke` for the owner. The owner's private keys are written and read
    only outside the checkout;
  - `keygen` and `issue` refuse a placeholder such as `<your name>`, pasted as it stands, as `--to` or `--org`.
- A licence is signed with Ed25519 by one of the two public keys the product ships, primary and recovery. A
  licence with no end date is perpetual, and only those keys can sign one.
- A signed revocation list in the public repository `farstic/snowarch-licences` (`engine.config.json` →
  `licence.revocations`):
  - fetched by git from that address, never from `origin`;
  - its version only goes up, and a lower one is ignored;
  - refreshed by `upgrade` and `upgrade --check` when a licence is installed;
  - refreshed in the background at every live server start with a licence installed;
  - `licence show` and `licence check` say when the held list is more than a day old.
- Under `SNOW_LICENCE_ENFORCE="true"`, a missing, invalid, expired or revoked licence refuses every MCP tool
  with `LICENCE_NOT_VALID`, the instance-free ones included. It also refuses every CLI command except
  `licence`, `doctor`, `status`, `version` and `upgrade`. A design-only licence refuses every tool of a live
  server.
- An audit line names the licence it was written under, only when that licence's signature holds.
- The licensing documents (ARC-11-C2):
  - `LICENSING.md`, `TRADEMARKS.md`, the Contributor Licence Agreement `CLA.md`, and `CONTRIBUTORS.md`;
  - `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` gains what a contribution needs: a signed CLA, failing tests first, and what is not
    accepted;
  - `NOTICE` names the project's contact;
  - every source file the author owns opens with a two-line copyright header.
- T-28 in `tests/VALIDATION-TESTS.md`, the owner's two-machine licence test, run by hand, and its first record,
  `docs/validation/2.0.12-rc.1-licence-sitting.md` (ARC-11-C4).
- T-29 in `tests/VALIDATION-TESTS.md`, live verification, run by hand on a live instance, with T-29b for a bare PDI
  (ARC-09-C117).
- T-30 in `tests/VALIDATION-TESTS.md`: co-firing gateways write both Envelopes in the same turn, in normal mode
  (ARC-09-C118).

### Changed

- A co-firing gateway now writes its five-part Envelope in the same turn, and the Envelopes are reconciled before
  the turn ends. "Reconcile before dispatch" is gone from `CLAUDE.md` and the gateway skills. Each Envelope cites as
  a single-gateway turn does, never a bare file name, and has its own grounding budget, eight pages by default
  (ARC-09-C118).
- `CLAUDE.md` and the six gateway skills gain one paragraph (ARC-09-C117). In live mode, before an Envelope is
  final, the Architect reads each "verify on the instance" item that is an instance fact, with read-only calls:
  eight per Envelope by default, with no cap under the opt-in. It cites each one "measured on instance <label>, <date>".
  Design-only is unchanged.
- `CLAUDE.md` gains one rule. With `enforced` and a licence state of `missing`, `invalid`, `expired` or
  `revoked`, the Architect produces no deliverable: it names the state and points to
  `./snowarch licence check`. Without enforcement, the `Licence:` line is information. In design-only this
  is a rule, not a lock.
- The doctor, `./snowarch status` and the `/snowarch` skill's sample count E-31's warning, which every
  checkout without a licence carries.
- `./snowarch status` prints the banner's `Licence:` line second, in every state, `ok` included (ARC-11-C1).
  The owner's rc.1 sitting found the panel silent with a licence in force. The `/snowarch` skill's sample,
  the status template and T-07 carry the line.
- A call the licence refuses writes one audit line, a read or a write alike, naming the tool, the instance
  label, the licence's id or `missing`, and the state that refused it. It carries no argument and no value
  (ARC-11-C1). The rc.1 sitting found the trail empty after two refusals.

### Fixed

- `./snowarch upgrade` refuses before anything moves, by name, when an untracked file is in the release's way: git
  refuses to overwrite one even with identical content, and the owner's rc.3 upgrade stopped at U5 with no reason. The
  preflight names the modified files, and a failure while the tree moves quotes git's own reason; "diverged" is said
  only when git refused the fast-forward (ARC-09-C120).
- `snow_atf_atf_step_add` refuses a value longer than its input variable's `max_length` before any write. It lists
  every input row of the step, including the rows the instance created from the config's defaults, and reports a mandatory
  input left without a value (ARC-09-C122).

### Added

- snowarch: the owner's two public keys in both key modules; the guard holds two (ARC-11-C1) (3a3e83b)
- snowarch: the server's licence gate at the router, its notice, audit id, refresh (ARC-11-C1) (e9a2215)
- snowarch: doctor E-31, the CLI refusing under enforcement, the banner licence line (ARC-11-C1) (f65fcde)
- snowarch: ./snowarch licence, the checkout's state and the revocation list's fetch (ARC-11-C1) (2f5ca3d)
- snowarch: the licence core and an empty key list, in the CLI and the server (ARC-11-C1) (df71237)

Tag v2.0.12 · contract 0c1efdfd4568 · docs-pin 68c0d11

## 2.0.11 — 2026-10-10

### Notes

**This release makes a write report a value the platform cut and refuse one longer than its column before it
is sent, runs and authors ATF tests through the interfaces the documentation describes, and tells the
Architect to ask, when no engagement is named, which engagement a first write to an instance is for.** There
is no migration step: an existing checkout keeps its instance store, its preset and its flags untouched, and
the documentation corpus stays pinned at `68c0d11`. The server has 399 tools, two more than 2.0.10
(`snow_atf_atf_test_add` and `snow_atf_atf_step_add`). The error registry gains four codes (68 to 72):
`VALUE_TRUNCATED`, `FIELD_NOT_STORED` and `VALUE_NOT_AS_SENT` are warnings on a write that succeeded, not
errors, and `VALUE_TOO_LONG` refuses a write before it is sent. The roster is supported on Claude Sonnet or
a more capable model; smaller models are not supported. One command can stop a script that used to finish:
`./snowarch upgrade` without `--yes` now stops when its input is closed (below).

**The release candidates, and what a live instance has confirmed.** `v2.0.11-rc.1` (`b661cf4`),
`v2.0.11-rc.2` (`d331001`) and `v2.0.11-rc.3` (`19d01ed`) were cut before this release. On rc.1, a Business
Rule was created in one write with its filter, the Advanced switch and `active` read back as sent, and a
41-character name came back as 40 with `VALUE_TRUNCATED`, `column_limit: 40` and `confirmed: true`, on the
POST and on the PATCH. The ATF exec fix shipped in rc.2 and was confirmed on rc.3: a suite ran through the
CI/CD API to a successful result. ATF test and step authoring was confirmed on rc.3: a test, and a step
with its script, were written and read back as sent. The refusal of a too-long value before the write was
confirmed on rc.3: an inherited column's limit was read on the table that defines it, and a value one
character over it was refused, with nothing written. The Batch API's Base64 bodies are checked against the
documentation page, not yet against an instance. The live procedures are in
`packages/snowarch/tests/live/README.md`.

**What a user of the MCP tools sees.**

- **A Business Rule is created in one write.** `snow_scr_business_rule_add` takes `filter_condition` (an
  encoded query) and `advanced` (default true) beside `active`, and sends them in one POST, the filter
  whenever one is given. The filter goes in the create, so the rule is not left without it between two
  calls, and the write gate asks once. `active` and `advanced` must be true or false: the string `"false"`
  used to create a live rule, and that or any other non-boolean is now refused with `INVALID_REQUEST`. `when`
  must be one of before, after, async or display, and `order` a whole number (a string such as `"50"` is
  refused); an `order` of 0 is sent as 0, where it used to become 100, and 100 is the default only when
  `order` is omitted or null.
- **A write reports a value the platform cut, and the session reports it before anything else.** When the
  platform answers a write with only the first part of a string it was sent, for example a name cut at its
  column's length, the result carries `warnings`, as its first key, with a `VALUE_TRUNCATED` entry: the field,
  the lengths sent and stored, the limit (the stored length when the dictionary states none) and whether the
  dictionary confirmed it. A Business Rule add whose answer does not show the filter it sent, or the Advanced
  switch it set to true, carries `FIELD_NOT_STORED`, after the record's own fields. The Business Rule add's
  success line names the name the platform stored. The check covers every tool that writes through the
  client's create and update calls, `snow_fluent_request_batch` on Table API paths, the file name of an
  attachment upload and a `createChangeRequest` method that no tool calls yet; the Now Assist and catalogue
  calls, fields the answer does not echo and values that are not strings are not checked, so an absent
  `warnings` key proves nothing. A result carries at most ten warnings: ten whole or, from eleven, the first
  nine whole and one roll-up for the rest. The always-loaded rule file tells a session to report each warning
  in full before it does anything else, and says the correcting modify is its own write with its own "write
  approved". In `snow_orch_playbook_exec` each step lists, uncapped, the cut values its own writes stored, and
  under `on_error: "stop"`, the default, the playbook halts after a step that stored a cut value or returned a
  warning, with `halted_at_step` and `halt_reason` (a step that stored one and then failed stops it as an error
  step); `"continue"` and `"skip"` go on. The audit line carries the warning codes, the tables, the field
  names and a count, never a value.
  **The premise was confirmed on rc.1:** the answer to a POST and to a PATCH on `sys_script` carries the value
  the platform stored. The check can only see a cut that the answer shows. The names `filter_condition` and
  `advanced` for the `sys_script` columns are not printed in the bundled corpus; on rc.1 a read-back showed
  both set, and `FIELD_NOT_STORED` is what catches it where they are not.
- **A string longer than its column is refused before it is sent.** Before a record is created or updated,
  the server reads each string's column from `sys_dictionary`, on the record's table and then up the tables
  it extends to the one that defines the column, and refuses a longer value with `VALUE_TOO_LONG`: nothing
  is written, so no record and no update-set entry is left to correct. Lengths are counted in code points,
  and only a String column with a stated `max_length` is judged. When the account cannot read the dictionary
  (it needs `personalize_dictionary`), or the read fails or takes more than three seconds, the write goes
  ahead and a cut is still reported as `VALUE_TRUNCATED`. A refusal after an earlier write of the same call
  names the records the call already wrote. The Batch API, attachments and change requests are not checked
  before the write. The `super_class` column the walk reads is not printed in the bundled corpus, and on
  rc.3 the refusal held against an instance: a 161-character short description was refused against `task`'s
  160, and nothing was written.
- **A 403 is not retried.** A 403 now fails at once from one request, with `INSUFFICIENT_PRIVILEGES`
  (`DELETE_ACL_DENIED` on a delete). Before, under the default policy, the same request went out four times
  with 1 s, 2 s and 4 s between them, about 7 s, to be refused each time. A 429, a 5xx, a request that never
  arrived and every other status that was retried before still are.
- **`snow_fluent_request_batch` follows the Batch API's Base64 bodies and `unserviced` list as the
  documentation describes them.** Each operation's body goes out Base64-encoded and each answer's body comes
  back decoded, as JSON when it is JSON and as text otherwise; callers no longer receive Base64 in
  `results[].body` for a JSON or text answer, and a body that is not UTF-8 text is returned as it came. The
  answer always carries `unserviced`, the ids of the operations the platform did not run because the batch
  reached a size or processing limit, and a `note` when it is not empty. It still carries only `id`,
  `status_code` and `body` for each item. This is checked against the documentation page, not against an
  instance: that the platform accepts the encoded body is not yet confirmed live. The tool's input schema still
  declares `body` as an object and every item is sent as `application/json`, so an XML body is not supported
  through the tool.
- **`snow_disco_table_discover` returns the table's own and inherited columns.** In the tests, against a fake
  dictionary, `incident` comes back with `short_description`, `number` and `state`, which `task` is expected to
  define, because the parent chain is walked; the dictionary is read in pages and not cut at 200 rows; a
  `max_length` the dictionary does not state is `null`, not 255; the cache is kept per instance, so a second
  instance is no longer answered with the first one's columns. An inherited column carries its parent's
  `mandatory`, `read_only` and `default_value`: a dictionary override on the child is not read. An answer cut
  short because the hierarchy could not be read, or because the table has more than 5,000 dictionary rows,
  carries a `note` and is not cached. That `sys_db_object.super_class` holds the parent, that a blank
  `max_length` means none is stated, and the shape of `internal_type` and `reference` (passed through as the
  instance sends them) are not printed in the bundled corpus and not yet confirmed live.
- **ATF suites run through the CI/CD API.** `snow_atf_atf_suite_exec` starts the suite with the documented
  `POST /api/sn_cicd/testsuite/run`, waits for it within `budget_seconds` (300 by default), and returns the
  outcome, the test counts and the suite's result record. The platform documents no call that runs a single
  test, so `snow_atf_atf_test_exec` runs the one suite that holds the test and says so; a test in no suite, or
  in several, is refused and nothing is created. Both used to fail with `INVALID_REQUEST` and "Requested URI
  does not represent any resource": they posted to `/api/now/atf/runner/run_*`, which the platform does not
  serve. The account needs the `sn_cicd.sys_ci_automation` or `admin` role. `snow_atf_atf_tests_index` with
  `suite_sys_id` lists the tests the suite holds, read from its Test Suite Tests rows; it could list every
  active test as the suite's own.
- **ATF tests and steps can be authored, and every record is read back.** `snow_atf_atf_test_add` and
  `snow_atf_atf_step_add` create a test and its steps, and a step's script is proven attached to the step. A
  value the instance stored differently comes back in `warnings[]`, under `VALUE_NOT_AS_SENT`,
  `VALUE_TRUNCATED` or `FIELD_NOT_STORED`. A Run Server Side Script step also needs `SCRIPTING_ENABLED`.
- **`./snowarch upgrade` does not proceed on a closed stdin.** At `Proceed? [Y/n]` a closed input (a pipe that
  had ended, `< /dev/null`, a CI step) was taken as a yes and the checkout was moved. It now prints `upgrade:
  no answer on stdin (it is closed, or not a terminal) — nothing was changed; pass --yes to accept the plan
  without asking` and exits 2. A script that ran `upgrade` without `--yes` and relied on that now needs
  `--yes`. An answer that is not Enter, `y` or `yes` now also changes nothing and exits 0, as `n` always did;
  any other word used to proceed.

**What a consultant sees.**

- **The specialists name what the corpus names.** The HRSD specialist no longer lists five notification names
  that are not in the bundled corpus as baseline; it says the baseline HR case notification names are not
  documented there and to verify them on the instance. The FSO specialist names the Personal Lines Claims
  adjuster, processor and manager roles, and the CMDB & CSDM specialist lists `cmdb_ci_cluster` and
  `cmdb_ci_cluster_node` as baseline cluster classes. The ITSM gateway says that the name of the field linking
  a problem to a change request is not documented in the bundled corpus, and the citation format of the six
  domain gateways now asks for a page's full path from `markdown/`, never a bare file name.
- **One engagement file.** Phase 1 Step 2 reads `clients/<name>/<name>-engagement-state.md`, the file
  onboarding creates; the Story Writer, Technical Designer, HLD/LLD Writer and Now Assist Specialist read its
  Roles section when they are pointed to the file. There is no separate instructions file: a role matrix kept
  in an old `<client>-instructions-v*.md` is no longer read, so move it into the Roles section.
- **`clients/_unfiled/`.** When no engagement is named, the design artefacts a session produces are saved
  under `clients/_unfiled/`, in `design-only` and `live` mode alike, never in a scratchpad or temp directory,
  and the reply says where they went. An instance change is never filed there.
- **The engagement question.** In a live session with no engagement named, the Architect asks once, before
  the session's first write to an instance and after the capabilities pre-flight has passed, `Which engagement
  is this for?`. The answer is the engagement in the update-set name, `<engagement>-<topic>`, and the engagement
  whose state file records the change. It is not a write approval; the "write approved" question still
  follows. What a session does when the user declines to name one is not ruled: the rule says to wait, so no
  write is made. Whether the question fires at `snow_core_instance_switch`, which writes nothing, is an open
  row: the line says "first mutating call", and the rule file counts that tool as one.
- **OPEN QUESTION ids carry their artefact.** Every id a format prescribes is `OQ-<kind>-<n>`, with the kinds
  ES, ST, DS, EV, DC and CN defined once in `governance/governance-rules.md`, so an answer no longer holds two
  questions that are both a bare `OQ-1`, and an id quoted outside its artefact is followed by that artefact's
  name. The Architect also keeps the text of a prompt it wrote for a helper, and an error its own tooling
  raised about that prompt, out of the answer; a remedy line from the rule file is still printed.
- **Grounding is a budget the answer declares.** Eight documentation pages by default; past that, the
  gateway says what is still unverified and what it will open, and continues. Parallel readers ground an
  Envelope only under Ultracode or max effort (below).
- **An Envelope is the engagement's context.** A gateway Envelope for a named engagement is saved under
  `clients/<engagement>/envelopes/`, and every later session in that engagement starts from it. Under
  Ultracode the Architect says how many workflows it will run and reports each one (below).
- **No session has a web tool.** The sub-agents lost theirs, and the main thread is denied them; they ground in
  the bundled documentation, and a counterparty's API documentation is supplied as a file or as pasted text
  (below).
- **What real sessions were seen doing.** On rc.1 the owner ran real sessions on Claude Sonnet 5.5, and they
  followed the new lines:
  - the §1.1 halt, with no field list and no design;
  - the declaration that parallel readers read the corpus;
  - `OQ-EV` ids;
  - design work filed under `clients/_unfiled/`;
  - two co-firing gateways reconciled in one Envelope;
  - in the live sitting, the engagement question before the first write.

  On the same prompts an Envelope took about a minute in normal mode, where 2.0.9 took 47 minutes to an hour.
  Under Ultracode one Envelope took 3 h 14 min, nearly all of it model generation. The numbers are in
  `docs/validation/2.0.11-rc.1-grounding-sitting.md`.

  On rc.3, in the live sitting, the write gate refused an approval that named no action (twice), the write
  question carried the suite's name, its sys_id and the budget, the capabilities were re-read before the write,
  and the session listed its extra read-only calls afterwards.

  Not yet seen in a real session: the up-front workflow count and the Envelopes saved under
  `clients/<engagement>/envelopes/` (T-26), the sub-agents without a web tool (T-27), and the live case of T-25.

### Fixed

- A write that the platform answers with a cut value is now reported when its answer shows the cut: a
  `VALUE_TRUNCATED` entry in `warnings`, the first key of the result. This covers every tool that writes
  through the client's create and update calls, the batch tool on Table API paths and an attachment's file
  name; on rc.1 it was seen for a POST and a PATCH on `sys_script`. The Now Assist and catalogue calls are
  not compared.
- An argument a tool does not declare is refused with `INVALID_REQUEST`, naming it and the arguments the tool
  takes, before anything is sent. It used to be dropped silently, so a caller could believe it had filtered or
  acted: an undeclared `active: false` on a create tool made a live record. Every tool's schema now says
  `additionalProperties: false`. A per-call `instance`, which was ignored, is refused with its own message: a
  write meant for another instance would have landed on the current one.
- A write no longer sends a string longer than its column when the dictionary can be read: it is refused
  with `VALUE_TOO_LONG` before anything is written.
- `snow_scr_business_rule_add` no longer creates a live rule from `active: "false"` (it refuses that and any
  `active` or `advanced` that is not a boolean), no longer turns an `order` of 0 into 100, and refuses before
  it writes a `when` the form does not offer, an `order` that is not a whole number (a string such as `"50"`
  included) and a `filter_condition` that is not a string.
- A 403 is no longer retried three times with a 7 s wait at the defaults. It fails at once, as
  `INSUFFICIENT_PRIVILEGES` (`DELETE_ACL_DENIED` for a delete).
- `snow_disco_table_discover` no longer leaves out inherited columns when the hierarchy can be read (checked
  against a mock, not yet live), stops at 200 dictionary rows (the ceiling is now 5,000 and a cut answer says
  so), turns an unstated `max_length` into 255, or answers one instance from another's cache. A `table` that
  is not a plain identifier is refused with `INVALID_REQUEST`.
- `snow_fluent_request_batch` no longer sends a JSON body the Batch API documents as Base64, no longer returns
  Base64 for a body it should decode, and no longer leaves the operations the platform did not run out of its
  answer.
- `snow_atf_atf_suite_exec` and `snow_atf_atf_test_exec` no longer fail with `INVALID_REQUEST` and
  "Requested URI does not represent any resource"; they run through the CI/CD API. A refusal names its cause:
  the role, an API this instance does not serve, a suite that does not exist, or the platform's own reason for
  not starting the run.
- `snow_atf_atf_tests_index` with `suite_sys_id` no longer filters tests on a column the documentation does
  not show, which could list every active test as the suite's own.
- `./snowarch upgrade`, with or without `--to <tag>`, no longer moves the checkout when its input is closed;
  it exits 2.
- The user guide no longer links four documents that do not exist, and three links in `docs/decisions/` and
  `docs/spikes/` that were at the wrong depth point at their pages.
- The specialists no longer give a model names the corpus does not contain: five HR notification names, the
  three Personal Lines Claims roles and two cluster classes that were missing (above), and eight
  table-and-field claims in four skill files, seven of them now in the corpus's own words, five with a page
  citation, and one marked as not documented in the corpus.
- Phase 1 Step 2 and the four builders that read a role matrix no longer read an instructions file that
  onboarding never wrote, and the Architect is now told to file work with no engagement under
  `clients/_unfiled/` and not in a session scratchpad, where it was lost at the next restart; no session has
  yet been seen to do so.
- A question id now says which kind of artefact it belongs to: an engagement-state question and a story's are
  `OQ-ES-1` and `OQ-ST-1`, where both were `OQ-1`; two artefacts of one kind are told apart by the artefact's
  name quoted after the id. No example or format in the roster numbers a question `OQ-1` any more, and a test
  fails on a bare one.
- A full `npm test` no longer leaves four `w17-win32-*` directories in the temporary directory, and no test
  file in `tests/`, `tools/snowarch/tests/` and `packages/snowarch/tests/` makes a temporary directory outside
  `tempDir()` or `trackTempDir()`, except the one file that tests the helper itself.

### Changed

- Under `on_error: "stop"`, the default, `snow_orch_playbook_exec` halts after a step that stored a cut value
  or returned a warning, and each step shows the cut values of its own writes in `warnings`, tagged with the
  step and the tool.
- The always-loaded rule file has a section on warnings (one sentence, then a line for `FIELD_NOT_STORED`
  and one for `VALUE_TRUNCATED` and `VALUE_NOT_AS_SENT`) and one more bullet in §2.1, the engagement
  question. The rule file is now 72 of its 72 lines.
- `snow_fluent_request_batch`'s description says what it sends and returns, names 50 operations as this tool's
  own cap and no longer says it "reduces round-trips by 50-70%", which nothing in the corpus states.
- An answer to the upgrade prompt that is not Enter, `y` or `yes` now changes nothing and exits 0; it used to
  go ahead unless it began with `n`.
- `./snowarch docs verify` also checks dotted names such as `hr_case.opened`, and CamelCase names on a line
  that says baseline, and prints `dotted names:` and `camelcase names:` beside the `identifiers:` line.
- The validation runner runs every case of `tests/VALIDATION-TESTS.md`, 27 of them now, as design-only
  sessions (the live cases as their dormant variants; the live variants are not run). It is written to run
  each session with only the checkout's project settings and hooks, no MCP server and a fixed tool set,
  prints for each turn the skills invoked, how many corpus pages were read and the cited pages that are
  missing, ambiguous or given by a bare file name, and runs a case's design-only Setup in the clone before
  the case (only T-25's has anything to run: it creates the `acme` engagement file); a Setup that needs an
  instance is not run, and one it cannot classify or that fails stops its case. Its flags are checked
  against `claude --help` and its text; no case has been run through it yet.
- The documentation link check covers the decision records, the spike write-ups, the user guide and four more
  top-level pages, and it fails for a top-level page that is neither checked nor excused; `docs/plans/` stays
  unchecked. The sweeps in `tests/no-legacy-surfaces.test.mjs` read each file they cover whole; only the
  changelog keeps a history boundary there.
- A gateway grounds its Envelope on a budget it declares: eight documentation pages by default, and past
  that it says which construct is still unverified and what it will open, then continues. Parallel readers
  ground an Envelope only in a session where the user has enabled Ultracode or max effort, and the answer
  says so.
- Under Ultracode or max effort, the Architect says up front how many workflows it will run and roughly how
  long, and prints a line as each one completes. It runs a second review round only when the first changed a
  verdict, a table, a citation or an open question. The run itself is not capped. A gateway Envelope for a
  named engagement, in any mode, is saved under `clients/<engagement>/envelopes/` and listed in the
  engagement's state file, and every later session in that engagement starts from it.
- The nine specialist sub-agents no longer have a web fetch tool: they ground in the bundled ServiceNow
  documentation. The Integration Specialist reads a counterparty's API documentation only when it is
  supplied, as a file or as pasted text; given only a URL, it asks for the document.
- WebFetch and WebSearch are denied to every session in a checkout, through the generated
  `.claude/settings.json`: the main Architect thread, and the skills it loads, have no web tool either. A deny
  entry of your own is kept.

### Added

- `snow_atf_atf_test_add` and `snow_atf_atf_step_add`, which create an ATF test and its steps and read every
  record back.
- `filter_condition` and `advanced` on `snow_scr_business_rule_add`; `warnings` on write results;
  `VALUE_TOO_LONG`, an error raised before a write that would not fit; `unserviced`, and a `note` when it is
  not empty, on `snow_fluent_request_batch`; a `note` on a `snow_disco_table_discover` answer that lacks
  inherited columns or stops at 5,000 dictionary rows.
- Seven platform notes in `docs/PLATFORM-NOTES.md`: PN-10, a Business Rule's name cut at 40 characters
  without an error, and what a write's answer carries (confirmed on rc.1); PN-11, the Batch API's Base64
  bodies and `unserviced_requests` list (on the documentation page, not yet seen on an instance); PN-12 to
  PN-14, what rc.1 showed of an update set's entries and of a stored `filter_condition`; PN-15, what an
  instance answers for a path it does not serve (the fix confirmed on rc.3); and PN-16, the input rows of a
  Test Step, which its insert creates from the step config's defaults and which go with the step (seen on
  rc.3). PN-07 no longer says the Developer skill keeps a name to 40 characters, which it never did.
- The live procedures for the cut-value premise, the ATF exec and authoring tools and the refusal before the
  write, in `packages/snowarch/tests/live/README.md`.
- A test that fails, naming the file and line, when a test file in the three test trees calls `mkdtemp` or
  `tmpdir()` instead of `tempDir()`; the helper's own test file is exempt.
- A test that fails when a sub-agent's tool list, or its instructions, name a web tool.
- The engagement question, `clients/_unfiled/` and the `OQ-<kind>-<n>` scheme (above).
